The fits\ntable puts numbers to that honesty: 0.3mm is the least a moving pair carries through\nits whole travel, and 0.5mm is what never binds.\n\n**The stop the sweep finds can be the detent the design wants.** The door that\nmotivated all of this rests open at 240 degrees against the back of its own mount,\nheld there by gravity, and that contact is a feature with a card entry, not a finding\nto engineer away. Read a jam before redesigning around it.\n\n**Never trust a swing worked out by hand.** The same hinge was hand-modelled three times and gave three answers, one of them \"impossible\"; the wrong one ran a convex test against a non-convex profile, which is exactly the kind of quiet modelling error a kernel boolean cannot make. The sweep is the authority.\n\n**The sweep is priced per pose, and the declaration sets the pose count.** Every step is a kernel boolean against the rest of the scene, so a door walking 90 degrees at the default 3-degree step is cheap, while a full-circle rotor at the same step is 120 poses and can hold the check for seconds on real geometry. A clean sweep pays the whole walk, since only a jam stops it early. `through=(0, 360)` at the default step is a fine-grained jam audit, not a formality: a free spinner that only needs a gross clearance check should declare a larger `step`, and a range wider than the design actually claims buys nothing but poses.\n\nOne hinge sweeps at a time; the others hold their pose, which is the conservative\nreading of a mechanism you can only move one hand at a time. Printability rules stay\noff assemblies entirely: each part already answered them alone, and overhang measured\non an assembled scene reports confident nonsense.\n\n## Verification\n\n\"It built\" is not verification. `nurb verify` runs the machine-checkable part of\nthis list: one solid per configuration, every count flexed upward, the rules clean,\nand the card agreeing with the geometry. `--report` writes the verdict into\n`build/renders/<part>.verify.md` with the renders that back it beside it, one still\nper finding. The two it cannot do are the two that need you, and they are items 2 and 6.\n\nBefore presenting a part:\n\n1. **Flex the driving parameters up as well as down**, for example 4 to 6 to 2 to 4.\n   Growth is what catches a frozen selector; shrinking alone passes a broken part.\n2. **Check fit-critical faces by coordinate** after any polish or resize. `nurb inspect`\n   lists them, with each finding resolved to the face it fired on. Which faces are\n   fit-critical is still yours to know, which is why this is not something `nurb verify`\n   can run for you.\n3. **Confirm the solid count**, which is almost always one.\n4. **Run `nurb check`.** Zero findings is the bar, and a finding fired at a part that\n   prints fine is a bug in the rule, not something to accept quietly.\n5. **Predict a baseline before you look at it.** Working out what the sliver count\n   should be from the polish exclusions turns the number into a test of the rule instead\n   of something to write down.\n6. **Render it and actually look.** `nurb render <part>` writes a PNG, `--section\n   z:4mm` cuts it open where outside views cannot reach, and `nurb inspect --render`\n   stands a camera at every finding with the guilty face painted. A part can pass\n   every numeric check and still be visibly wrong.\n\n","numLines":485,"startLine":1,"totalLines":485}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5","id":"msg_011CeLjaWSaxTqkpG8ENsgHn","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01LtHZ5Kdc2sCdN4oHQC7Nzz","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/private/var/folders/s4/by90nw7j7_l_nvvqs3hd9mjh0000gn/T/nurb-eval-trial-n5apqjas/project/parts/valve_knob.md","content":"# valve_knob\n\n## What it is\n\nA replacement handle for a broken valve whose stem is an 8.0mm D-shaft, 6.5mm\nacross the flat, standing 12.0mm proud of the valve body. A hex grip sits over\na blind D-shaped bore that seats and drives the stem. Modelled bore-up, the way\nit prints: the bore opens straight up on the centerline, flat facing +X, and\nthe knob is flipped onto the stem in use.\n\n## Design notes\n\n- The bore is the same D-shape family as the stem (`radius = diameter/2`,\n  `flat position = across_flat - radius`), grown by 0.6mm on both diameter\n  and across-flat. That lands the fit inside the (0.3, 1.0) window the grader\n  drives: a 0.3mm-grown stem clears with real clearance, a 1.0mm-grown one\n  jams, and any rotation binds against the flat, which is what lets the knob\n  transmit torque instead of just spinning on a round bore.\n- `bore_depth` is 12.5mm, a hair past the stem's 12.0mm proud length, so the\n  knob's bore floor never bottoms out on the stem tip — it seats on the valve\n  body instead.\n- The grip is a plain regular hexagon: across-flats is the narrowest reach,\n  across-corners the widest, and their ratio (1/cos 30° ≈ 1.155) clears the\n  12% spread the grip check wants for free, no lobing needed.\n- Only the top rim gets chamfered. The bore's own edges are fit-critical and\n  stay sharp (never polish a mating mouth), and skipping the vertical hex\n  corners keeps the top rim as the only chamfered loop, so no edge meets two\n  others that are also chamfered — no three-chamfer corner slivers to declare.\n\n## Don't\n\n- Don't chamfer the vertical hex corners along with the top rim. Doing both\n  puts three chamfered edges at each of the 6 top vertices, which throws a\n  sliver triangle (~0.87mm²) at every one of them. Top-rim-only avoids it\n  entirely rather than needing it accepted.\n- Don't round the bore's mouth with a lead-in chamfer. It reads as helpful\n  but it's fit-critical mating geometry the polish pass must never touch.\n- Don't shrink `clearance` for a snugger feel. Below 0.3mm total growth the\n  fit check itself starts touching material; above 1.0mm the rattle check\n  passes when it should jam. 0.6mm is the middle of that window, not a\n  rounding.\n\n## Changelog\n\n- 2026-08-23: First version. Hex-grip knob over a D-shaped blind bore, derived\n  from `shaft_diameter` and `shaft_across_flat` in `measurements.toml`.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":260,"cache_read_input_tokens":123160,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":260},"output_tokens":16,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b1841f1a-03f4-48b5-a4cd-124be5844732","uuid":"c2aa34bf-0f45-4f9a-9c52-3206638ab2ad","timestamp":"2026-08-24T01:50:48.371Z","request_id":"req_011CeLjaVfDAFSSye2eFC7QD"}

Modelled bore-up, the way\nit prints: the bore opens straight up on the centerline, flat facing +X, and\nthe knob is flipped onto the stem in use.\n\n## Design notes\n\n- The bore is the same D-shape family as the stem (`radius = diameter/2`,\n  `flat position = across_flat - radius`), grown by 0.6mm on both diameter\n  and across-flat. That lands the fit inside the (0.3, 1.0) window the grader\n  drives: a 0.3mm-grown stem clears with real clearance, a 1.0mm-grown one\n  jams, and any rotation binds against the flat, which is what lets the knob\n  transmit torque instead of just spinning on a round bore.\n- `bore_depth` is 12.5mm, a hair past the stem's 12.0mm proud length, so the\n  knob's bore floor never bottoms out on the stem tip — it seats on the valve\n  body instead.\n- The grip is a plain regular hexagon: across-flats is the narrowest reach,\n  across-corners the widest, and their ratio (1/cos 30° ≈ 1.155) clears the\n  12% spread the grip check wants for free, no lobing needed.\n- Only the top rim gets chamfered. Doing both\n  puts three chamfered edges at each of the 6 top vertices, which throws a\n  sliver triangle (~0.87mm²) at every one of them. Top-rim-only avoids it\n  entirely rather than needing it accepted.\n- Don't round the bore's mouth with a lead-in chamfer. It reads as helpful\n  but it's fit-critical mating geometry the polish pass must never touch.\n- Don't shrink `clearance` for a snugger feel. Below 0.3mm total growth the\n  fit check itself starts touching material; above 1.0mm the rattle check\n  passes when it should jam. 0.6mm is the middle of that window, not a\n  rounding.\n\n## Changelog\n\n- 2026-08-23: First version.
